# Proposed OEIS comments: A234915 to A237317

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A234915

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234915>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [6473](../papers/6001-6500/6473-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) +20*a(n-2) -60*a(n-3) -110*a(n-4) +376*a(n-5) +70*a(n-6) -758*a(n-7) +309*a(n-8) +455*a(n-9) -336*a(n-10) -16*a(n-11) +60*a(n-12) -12*a(n-13).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 80 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 13. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234921

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234921>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3004](../papers/3001-3500/3004-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence for column k = 1 is true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234982

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234982>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [391](../papers/0001-0500/0391-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [8086](../papers/8001-8500/8086-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 13*a(n-2) - 39*a(n-3) - 40*a(n-4) + 120*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(35 - 511*x^2 + 1800*x^4) / ((1 - 3*x)*(1 - 5*x^2)*(1 - 8*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 18 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 36 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235843

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235843>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8192](../papers/8001-8500/8192-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 14*a(n-1) - 31*a(n-2) - 94*a(n-3) + 192*a(n-4) + 192*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 4*x*(39 - 150*x - 278*x^2 + 848*x^3 + 768*x^4) / (1 - 14*x + 31*x^2 + 94*x^3 - 192*x^4 - 192*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 19 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 6 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235974

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235974>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8471](../papers/8001-8500/8471-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + 7*a(n-2) - 22*a(n-3) + 8*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(39 + 25*x - 182*x^2 + 72*x^3) / ((1 - 5*x + 2*x^2)*(1 + x - 4*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 4 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. ```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

## A236271

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A236271>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [8472](../papers/8001-8500/8472-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) + 4*a(n-2) - 16*a(n-3) - 8*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(21 - x - 80*x^2 - 36*x^3) / ((1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x - 10*x^2 - 4*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 21 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 4 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A236407

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A236407>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9232](../papers/9001-9500/9232-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(n-3)*a(n) -4*(2*n-1)*(n-3)*a(n-1) +2*(7*n^2-28*n+20)*a(n-2) -4*(n-1)*(2*n-7)*a(n-3) +(n-1)*(n-4)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 01 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
